**What the fifth review confirmed independently**, having had the diff and the stripped findings list only:
no new `app.*` GUC beyond C1's two; `set_config` called from one site with a literal `true`; no `BYPASSRLS`
granted or implied; no new cross-tenant path beyond the §4 list; no `RETURNING` anywhere in the diff and the
worker expiry statement untouched; **no second PHP mirror of tenant scope left anywhere** in `app/`,
`database/`, `config/` or `routes/` — the GUC's only other consumers are the RLS policies; a session-scoped
`app.bypass_tenant` on the app connection grants nothing, so `owner_bypass_*` really is owner-only; and
`composer.lock` untouched. It also measured that `ALTER ROLE qrpager_app SET app.tenant_id` is refused
`42501`, so M-1's producer is developer, operator or package code, never an outsider — which is why it was a
Medium and not a High before C2 made it blocking.

**One comment corrected by its own measurement.** `TenantContext`'s note that "a full `ROLLBACK` leaves the
setting empty" is true only when no session-scoped value sits underneath it; with one, a full `ROLLBACK`
leaves that value in place. The note now says so, and the condition is guaranteed by M-1's refusal rather
than assumed.

**Corrected 2026-10-26 (M-3, sixth review), and this one was a real regression, not just wrong prose.** The
sixth round moved the clear out of `setGuc()` into `clearScope()`, and the H-B durability test — the only thing
that had ever reached `setGuc()`'s `catch` — went with it. Measured: replacing that whole `catch` with
`throw $e;` left **183 passing**, so the site that carries the tenant id as a bound parameter had no
discriminating test at all, and §6's "a regression test that fails without the fix" was not met for it. The
claim that `insertBypassAudit()`'s catch was "the one uncovered line pair in the class" was false when written.

A test is added for it, using the one vector that lands there and nowhere else: a GUC name Postgres will read
and refuse to write — `current_setting('nodot', true)` returns NULL, so `readState()` succeeds, while
`set_config('nodot', …)` raises `42704`. It asserts the escaping message carries no statement, no bindings and
**not the tenant id**, which is what the raw `QueryException` would have interpolated. Reverted, it fails with
that raw exception.

**Still not proven, and now stated precisely:** `insertBypassAudit()`'s catch, for the reason recorded in the
fifth round. `readState()`'s `! is_object($row)` throw is also unexercised and is unreachable in practice,
since `current_user` always has a `pg_roles` row — it exists to fail closed rather than to be reached.

**A sixth review (2026-10-26), of the sixth round's fix diff (`afd0343..0ca929a`).** Given that diff and the
fifth review's findings list with the dispositions stripped, and told to look first for the shape three of the
last five rounds produced — _correct on a clean build, wrong on a machine carrying earlier state_ — under
Octane, where the connection and the `TenantContext` singleton both outlive a request.

**Verdict: no Critical, no High.** For the first time on this branch a review of the previous round's fixes
found nothing tag-blocking. It confirmed **all ten** fifth-review findings closed, four by its own measurement
rather than by reading the tests, and verified every revert proof this file claims — including that the H-C
refusal fails exactly 2 tests from `run()` and 1 from `withoutTenantScope()`, and that the L-2 revert fails
_only_ its own new test. It independently established that H-C's **narrower** rule is the correct narrower
rule for every reachable role: `qrpager_root` is the only `rolsuper`/`rolbypassrls` role, the other three are
`NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS NOINHERIT` with **zero** `pg_auth_members` rows, role attributes are not inherited,
and `SET row_security = off` fails closed with `42501` for the app **and** the owner. It also measured the
per-scope cost at **4 statements**, unchanged by the diff, confirming "zero extra round trips"; captured
`report()` output across all four new exception paths with 0 occurrences of `set_config`, `select `,
`current_setting`, `pg_roles` or any chained previous; and reproduced the fresh-clone gate at **183 passed**.

It raised **5 Medium and 6 Low**. Four are repaired in this round's follow-up commit and are recorded above
where the false claim was: the `setGuc` coverage regression (M-3), the four-not-three statement sites (L-6),
R-015's false compensating control (M-4), and R-016's imprecise premise (L-6). The rest await disposition in
§0. Two of its own claims are corrected here against measurement:

- **It called the role-mutating tests a regression introduced by this diff.** They are not: `grep` finds three
  sites, and two of them — `RuntimeRoleGuardTest` and `BootstrapDatabaseCommandTest`, the latter granting
  `superuser bypassrls` — date from `122d203`, the original Task 1 commit. Only one is this round's. It is
  pre-existing debt across three sites, which changes who owns it and when.
- **Its M-1 and M-2 are real and were reproduced here before being accepted**, on the same standard this file
  applies to every other finding: `current()` returning a session-scoped uuid with no scope open, and
  `run()` returning `'finished'` with the write gone and `transactionLevel()` left at 1 after a callback left
  a transaction open — with a second `withTenant` on the same singleton then **succeeding** by nesting into it.

**The seventh round (2026-10-26) — the sixth review's dispositions.** (a), (b) and (c) fixed on this branch
with revert-proven tests; (d) and (e) to `§5` as **R-017** and **R-018**. (c) was fixed here against the
builder's own recommendation to defer it: the stated dependency on R-010 was not real, since escaping text
before it is interpolated into a message needs no lint guard.

| #       | Fixed how                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Proven by revert                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **(b)** | `run()` captures `transactionLevel()` inside the transaction it opens and refuses to return unless the callback left it at that depth. A **shallower** depth is the connection going away underneath — a reconnect, or a `commit()` from inside the callback — reported as the scope being lost; a **deeper** one is a transaction the callback opened and never closed, which is what made Laravel's commit a no-op. The check runs **before** the clear, because the clear cannot see it: the GUC still reads correctly inside the still-open transaction. The connection is then unwound to depth 0 before the throw, so it does not return to the pool carrying an open transaction and a tenant setting. | The call removed → the dangling-transaction test fails. It asserts the H-B property in the same words as the other two routes to it, and additionally that the connection is left at depth 0 with no setting, and that the next scope on it is a scope rather than a savepoint inside the previous request's transaction.                                                                               |
| **(a)** | `current()` checks provenance before format: no entry in `$openScopes` for that connection, no answer — and no round trip. The two entry points can no longer disagree about one value, because the condition `run()` treats as a hard failure is the condition `current()` now answers `null` to.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Provenance check removed → 1 test fails. Format check removed → 1 test fails. **The format test had to be rewritten to keep its meaning:** with the provenance check in front of it, its original vector (a session-scoped non-uuid, no scope open) was answered by provenance, so it would have passed for a reason other than the one it names. It now sets the bad value from _inside_ a live scope. |
| **(c)** | Every message in `TenantContextException` that interpolates text this class did not produce — the setting read from the session, the role read from `current_user`, the caller's own rejected value — passes through one escaper first. `\p{C}` rather than a C0 range, so it also covers C1, the bidi overrides and the zero-width characters, which spoof a log line without needing a newline; invalid UTF-8 falls back to printable ASCII; length is capped; a uuid passes through untouched, because that is the legitimate case and these messages are useless without it.                                                                                                                              | The escaper neutered → the log-forging test fails. It asserts the message is **one line** by `preg_split('/\R/')`, which is the property; the forged _text_ survives as inert text on that line, and asserting its absence would have been asserting the wrong thing. A second test caps the message length against an 8 KiB setting.                                                                   |

**Two traps caught inside this round, before they reached this file.** Both are the same shape as the defect
the sixth review found — a fix quietly orphaning the proof of an older one — and they are recorded because the
record is what this branch gets wrong most often:

- **Adding (b)'s depth postcondition orphaned `clearScope()`'s read-back.** The depth check took over both
  routes the read-back used to catch, so deleting its comparison left **190 tests passing** — exactly how the
  sixth round orphaned `setGuc()`'s catch. The read-back is not redundant: it is the only thing that sees a
  **same-depth** divergence, where something inside the block wrote over `app.tenant_id` with a raw
  `set_config` and every query after that point ran under a tenant the caller never chose. It now has its own
  vector, its own exception naming what actually happened, and its own revert proof.
- **The first version of that new test was not discriminating either.** It asserted the H-B property
  ("either the write survives or something is raised"), which in the overwrite case is satisfied _trivially_ —
  the write does survive — and it passed with the read-back deleted. The property that matters there is the
  contract: a block either ran under its caller's tenant throughout, or it reports. The refusal is now the
  assertion and the rollback its consequence.

**A seventh review (2026-10-26), of `0ca929a..472ce51`.** Scoped wider than asked, deliberately: the brief
named the last commit, and `fd6fffd` had never been reviewed either while containing the `§4a` corrections
this review was asked to audit. It was told that **a false claim in this file is a finding of the same
severity as the thing it misdescribes**, because `§4a` had then asserted something measurement contradicts in
three consecutive rounds.

**It found 1 High, 4 Medium and 6 Low** — stated to match the table below it, after the first version of this sentence said "1 High and 5 Medium" and omitted the Lows entirely (S-3, eighth review: `3+2=5` double-counted the one Medium that was both). The High and three of the Mediums were in the seventh round's own work, and
two of the Mediums were false claims in this file. It confirmed the sixth review's M-1, M-3, M-4, L-1 and L-6
closed by its own reverts, verified every revert proof this file claims (including that each named test fails
and that nothing else does), and confirmed that the two "traps caught inside this round" recorded above are
genuine. It also confirmed `§4a`'s coverage paragraph is compliant and recorded no coverage figure of its own.

| #                       | Sev      | Finding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Fixed how                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Proven by revert                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **F1**                  | **High** | **The reset loop added for (b) unwound to depth 0 instead of to the depth the scope opened at, so a scope nested inside an application transaction destroyed the caller's transaction.** `§2.10` states every migration body is already at transaction level 1, so that is the ordinary case. Reproduced here before accepting it: the caller was left at depth **0**, its earlier write **gone**, its later writes autocommitted one at a time, and — because it caught the refusal, which is the shape `§2.10`'s own per-tenant loop uses and which this repo's tests endorse — its `transaction()` returned **`'outer-ok'` with nothing raised**. H-B's exact failure one level up, created by the fix for H-B's third route.                                                                                     | Unwind only what the callback added: `transactionLevel() > $depth`, never past it. `run()`'s own level is left to Laravel's `transaction()`, which rolls back exactly the level it opened when the throw reaches it.                                                                                                                                                                             | Bound changed back to `> 0` → the nested-in-an-application-transaction test fails, asserting the caller is left at its own depth and that both of its writes commit together.                                 |

**What the seventh review confirmed independently:** no new `app.*` GUC; every production `set_config` third
argument a literal `true`; no `BYPASSRLS` in application code; no new cross-tenant path; no `RETURNING` added
and the worker expiry path untouched; `composer.lock` untouched. On stale state across an Octane request, it
read `$openScopes` by reflection after every refusal path — dangling transaction, overwritten setting, callback
throw, cross-connection refusal — and found it `[]` in all four with both connections back at level 0, and a
missing entry failing **closed** for all three entry points. It named the two places that fail **open** on
stale state: `current()` with an in-scope overwrite (F4 → R-017), and `$depth` being captured _inside_ the
transaction, so the postcondition cannot see a transaction a previous request left behind. Against captured
log output across four refusal paths: **0** occurrences of `set_config`, `select `, `current_setting`,
`pg_roles`, the tenant id or even its first 14 characters, and `getPrevious()` null in every case — re-measured
with `zend.exception_ignore_args=0`. It also noted `Str::isUuid` carries the `/D` modifier, so the escaper's
uuid fast path cannot be bypassed with a trailing newline.

**One claim of this file's that the reviewer could not reconstruct, and said so:** the "left 190 tests
passing" figure in the trap note above describes an intermediate state during the seventh round that no commit
holds. It is consistent with the surrounding measurements but is not independently checkable, which is worth
knowing about any figure in this file that is not pinned to a commit.

**An eighth review (2026-10-26), of `472ce51..6f0d40a`, against the bar in §0.** **10 findings: 1 High, 2
Medium, 7 Low.** It confirmed `F1`, `F2`, `F5`, `F7`, `F8` and `F11` closed by its own reverts, verified the
gate figures by running the suite at `6f0d40a`, and re-confirmed the log-output property on the new refusal
paths — 61,599 bytes of captured Monolog output with **0** occurrences of `set_config`, `current_setting`,
`pg_roles`, `select `, `SAVEPOINT`, the tenant id or its first 14 characters, and `getPrevious()` null on all
five paths.

**S-1 met the bar, and the branch did not ship on it.** Measured here before accepting it, three routes
identically: inside `withTenant($alpha, …, 'pgsql_owner')`, with **no `set_config` anywhere**, the in-scope
probe reads `[1, 0]` — correctly narrowed — and then one ordinary callback call (`commit()`, `rollBack()` or
`DB::purge()`) empties the transaction-local GUC, so a nested `withTenant()` stops short-circuiting as
re-entry, takes the transaction path, and **its `finally` deletes the single shared `$openScopes` key while
the outer block is still running**. Both halves of the bypass guard then pass, and a bypass opened inside
that block read **2 of 2 tenants** and committed a keyed cross-tenant `UPDATE` — `Pacific/Auckland` on the
other tenant's row, read back from an independent superuser session, with the audit row present.

**One of the review's four routes does not reproduce, and the correction matters.** It reported
`(new TenantContext)->withoutTenantScope(…)` inside a live scope as silent and unrefused — its strongest
route, since `TenantContext` is not `final`. Measured: **refused**, with the other tenant's row unchanged.
With no derail, `app.tenant_id` is still in force on that connection, so the **GUC half** of the guard
fires. The guard has two halves and only one is per-instance, so the review's "access control resting on
mutable per-instance state" verdict (its ASVS V4.1.1 finding) is overstated.

**The ninth round — S-1 and S-6 fixed, everything else to §5.**

| #                                           | Fixed how                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Proven by revert                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **S-1**                                     | `$openScopes` is a **depth counter**: a connection name and a number, never a tenant id, so the forbidden mirror is not involved. An inner scope increments on the way in and **restores** the previous count on the way out instead of unsetting the key, so an inner scope can no longer erase the record of the scope running around it. All three derail routes close at once, because none of them is about the GUC any more.                         | The decrement replaced by the unconditional `unset()` → **exactly the three derail tests fail**, one per route, and nothing else. |
| **S-6**                                     | The bypass restores its GUC through a read-back, the way `run()`'s clear does, so a callback that commits and reopens — which leaves the depth matching while the transaction-local bypass GUC reverted at the `COMMIT` — is refused instead of reported as a success. Without it the rest of the block ran with **no** bypass and read 0 rows, which for a `§2.10` pattern-2 backfill is `H-1` exactly: a migration that does nothing and says it worked. | The read-back replaced by the plain restore → **exactly one test fails**, the commit-and-reopen test.                             |
| **S-2, S-3, S-4, S-5, S-7, S-8, S-9, S-10** | To `§5` as **R-022**, **R-023**, **R-024** and **R-025**, each naming its finding ids, with an owner and a date. S-3, S-9 and S-10 are corrected in place as well, since they are statements in this file rather than risks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | n/a                                                                                                                               |

**A trap caught inside this round, and it is the shape this project keeps producing.** The first version of
S-1's regression test filtered `audit_log` on a reason string that was constant per route. `audit_log` is
never truncated (**R-016**), so the test matched a row left by an **earlier revert run** — one made while the
vulnerability was live and the bypass therefore committed its audit row. The S-6 revert then "failed" three
tests it has nothing to do with. It was caught by refusing to report a revert proof whose failure count could
not be explained, and it is **R-016's predicted shape arriving inside the test written to prove the fix for
S-1**. The reason is now unique per run, and both reverts were re-measured from a **dropped and rebuilt**
database, one drop per revert.

### Task 2 — **complete, merged and tagged `task-2`** at `2e42267` (2026-10-27). Built on `task-2-api-core`. Ticket `QRPAGER-9`, epic E1

**The measured starting point, at `7f1c8f6`, before a line of this task's code existed.** Written here
first and deliberately, because `§4a`'s job is to carry what was measured and not what was hoped, and
because two of the three invariants this task ships on (`§3.1` row 2) are **false today**:

| Invariant this task ships on (`§3.1` row 2) | State at `7f1c8f6` | How that was measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A request without tenant context never reaches the DB | **Unenforced.** There is no HTTP surface and no guard; RLS returns 0 rows silently rather than refusing | `routes/web.php` holds one route, `/`. Nothing calls `assertSafeRuntimeRole()` |
| Logs carry no SQL, bound parameters or secrets | **FALSE** | `Log::error($e->getMessage(), ['exception' => $e])` for a real `QueryException` wrote **22 lines** containing the statement text *and* the bound uuid interpolated into it. Laravel builds that message itself, in `Connection::run()`'s exception path |
| Each named guard fails on a deliberately bad file | **No guard exists.** The C1/C2 repo guard is `R-010`; the three architecture guards are this task's | `composer.json` has `pint`, `stan`, `test`; CI adds gitleaks, migrate-twice, rollback-fingerprint and coverage. No step reads source files |

And the clause `§3.2` finding 2 says no gate anywhere names — error response **bodies** — measured at the
same commit, with `APP_DEBUG=true`, which `.env.example` ships:

| Response | What the body carried |
| --- | --- |
| JSON 500 for a `QueryException` | the full SQL **and** the bound uuid |
| JSON 500 for `UnsafeDatabaseRoleException` | **`qrpager_app`** and the attribute that made it unsafe — `R-021`, reproduced rather than inherited |
| HTML 500 for the same | 862,104 bytes carrying all of it |

That table is the baseline this task's gate evidence is measured against. A later "no SQL in logs" claim
here means nothing unless it is the same measurement, re-run, returning nothing.

**Runtime pin, and why the full tag.** The Octane server is
**`dunglas/frankenphp:1.13.3-php8.4-bookworm`**, pinned **by full tag, never `1.13.3`**. The bare tag is
ambiguous: `1.13.3-php8.5` and `1.13.3-php8.5.12` ship in the **same** FrankenPHP release, so the
unqualified tag can float onto a PHP this project does not run and would silently violate
`PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md §1`, which locks PHP 8.4. Task 0 already paid for this once — a skeleton file
instructing agents to install PHP 8.5 against `§1`, recorded above. Verified in-session:

```
$ docker run --rm dunglas/frankenphp:1.13.3-php8.4-bookworm php -v
PHP 8.4.27 (cli) (built: Oct 21 2026 15:55:55) (ZTS)
$ docker run --rm dunglas/frankenphp:1.13.3-php8.4-bookworm frankenphp version
FrankenPHP v1.13.3 PHP 8.4.27 Caddy v2.11.7
```

`1.13.3` is also the floor Octane v2.22.0 requires (`$requiredFrankenPhpVersion = '1.13.0'`), and
FrankenPHP's own 1.13.3 notes record worker mode broken for symlinked document roots in 1.13.0–1.13.2.
The build is **ZTS**, which is correct rather than incidental: worker mode is threaded, and an extension
compiled for NTS will not load in it.

**Extensions are installed explicitly, and a missing one fails the build.** Measured inside that image:
it ships `PDO`, `mbstring` and `posix`, and **not `pdo_pgsql`, `redis`, `pcntl`, `intl` or `bcmath`**.
The Dockerfile installs `pdo_pgsql`, `redis` and `pcntl` with `install-php-extensions` and then
**asserts** them, so an image that lacks one fails at build time instead of at runtime:

- `pdo_pgsql` — without it the application cannot reach Postgres at all.
- `redis` — the locked store for session, cache and queue (`§1`), and the rate limiter.
- `pcntl` — **not optional.** Laravel's `trap()` is how a long-running command handles `SIGTERM`, so
  **both** the gate's SIGTERM drain proof and the readiness carrier (`readiness:refresh`, run by
  `schedule:work`) depend on it. A missing `pcntl` does not error; the signal is simply never trapped,
  which is a drain that silently does not drain — the failure mode `§6`'s "fresh clone, not just a fresh
  DB" clause exists to catch.

**The three invariants, re-measured at `73a5d78`.** The baseline table above is what this is measured
against; a claim here means nothing unless it is the same measurement, re-run.

| Invariant (`§3.1` row 2) | State at `7f1c8f6` | State at `73a5d78` | The measurement |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A request without tenant context never reaches the DB | Unenforced | **Enforced, before execution** | `TenantScopeGuard` on `Connection::beforeExecuting()`, armed by a GLOBAL middleware so no route can be added without it. The test that distinguishes it from a listener does not rely on RLS: it deletes from `password_reset_tokens` — a global table the app role may delete from — with a subquery against `tenants`. Unarmed the row goes; with a `DB::listen` guard the row goes AND the exception is raised; with `beforeExecuting` the row survives |
| Logs carry no SQL, bound parameters or secrets | **FALSE** (22 lines with the statement and the bound uuid) | **True, at both ends** | `RedactingProcessor` via the `RedactLogs` tap on every channel, plus `ReportRedactedException` at the producer for the emergency logger no tap reaches. Asserted against the file the logger wrote, never a spy. Postgres puts `DETAIL: Failing row contains (...)` — every column value — AHEAD of Laravel's `SQL:` clause, so stripping that clause alone was not enough |
| Each named guard fails on a deliberately bad file | **No guard exists** | **Four do** | deptrac's three layers plus the C1/C2 repo guard, each driven against a deliberately-bad fixture that the real configuration rejects with a non-zero exit. A fourth deptrac rule (B5) stops a feature module being built on the pre-Task-5 auth stub |

**And `§3.2` finding 2's bodies, re-measured at the same commit:**

| Response | At `7f1c8f6` | At `73a5d78` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| JSON 500 for a `QueryException` | the full SQL **and** the bound uuid | one RFC 7807 document; the body contains neither, nor the relation name, nor the word SQLSTATE |
| JSON 500 for `UnsafeDatabaseRoleException` | `qrpager_app` and the attribute | a generic `server-error` document; the role and the reasons are in the LOG and absent from the body, both asserted |
| HTML 500 for the same | 862,104 bytes | under 500 bytes, at `APP_DEBUG=true`, because the renderer is registered for every request at any debug setting |

**The runtime, measured against the pinned image rather than the tag:**

```
FrankenPHP v1.13.3 PHP 8.4.27 Caddy v2.11.7
PHP 8.4.27 (cli) (ZTS)
in-flight response: 200 {"completed":true,"held_seconds":3.005,"pid":1}
exit code: 0

with Redis up:       /readyz = 200
with Redis down:     /healthz = 200   /readyz = 503   unknown path = 404
with Redis up again: /readyz = 200
```

The 404 is the line worth reading: that request goes through the rate limiter, which cannot reach Redis,
so 404 means it failed open. 500 would mean a `RedisException` reached the renderer, which is the default
behaviour the code replaces. Both proofs run in CI as their own job (Q7), because neither is reachable
from `php artisan test` — FrankenPHP's drain is Go-side and Octane's worker loop exits through
`frankenphp_handle_request()` returning false, so a test could only have asserted that the code which
would drain is present.

**Pool saturation, re-measured:** 40 `qrpager_app` connections held against the live cluster, the 41st
refused with **`08006`** and `FATAL: too many connections for role "qrpager_app"` — never `53300`, which
is the SQLSTATE anyone would guess and which a mapper keyed on it would wait for forever. With the pool
at 40/40 the worker-role probe still returned `{"app_backends":40,"app_limit":40,"saturation":1}`,
`/readyz` answered and `/healthz` returned 200.

**The suite:** 198 at `7f1c8f6` → 341 at `73a5d78` → **364 passed, 1247 assertions** after the review
fixes, against the composed Postgres 16 and Redis 7.

**Corrected 2026-10-27 (QA L-1).** This paragraph said the figure was "re-measured on a second machine at
`8075aec` and identical, so the figure is the code's and not one builder's" — and attached that claim to
the headline number. `QRPAGER-9-progress.md:408` is precise and says otherwise: *"The **240** figure was
re-measured on a second machine at `8075aec`"*. So the cross-machine check is real and it was done against
240, at a commit sixteen behind `73a5d78`; **no headline figure on this branch has ever been checked on a
second machine**, and 364 has not either. The sentence took a true claim about one number and moved it to
another, which is `R-025`'s category exactly — and it is the second time this round that a `§4a` sentence
was found claiming more than the measurement it rests on. What IS repeatable here: **thirteen** consecutive full
runs on this machine at 364 passed / 1247 assertions, and the same suite at 363 passed / 1 skipped / 1244
assertions inside the pinned runtime image — a different PHP build and a different filesystem, if not a
different host. The figure after the third round of fixes was **371 passed / 1280 assertions** at
`79f0882`, and **at head, after the fourth and last, it is 381 passed / 1321 assertions** — three
consecutive `composer gate` runs, all six steps green and exit 0 each time.

**The one red run is IDENTIFIED, 2026-10-27, and it is `R-049`.** This paragraph used to end "13 of 14
observed runs green, one unexplained", and the first review of the fix diff was right that a `§4a`
sentence is not a disposition: `§11`'s merge rule admits two — a fix with a regression test, or a `§5`
row with an owner and a date — and prose in this file is neither. The cause is **two concurrent
runners**. `TestCase::setUp()` truncates six tenant tables on `pgsql_owner` before every test and nothing
serializes that against a second `php artisan test`; `db:bootstrap` takes `pg_advisory_lock` and its own
comment says "parallel runners queue instead of racing a duplicate `CREATE ROLE`", so concurrency is
handled for the bootstrap and nowhere else. Reproduced **deterministically** at `ae43e12` by the reviewer,
from two `pest` invocations on two different files: `OwnerAndBypassTest` took `SQLSTATE[23503]` on
`tenant_users_tenant_id_fkey` — "Key is not present in table `tenants`", from `TestCase.php:305` — and
`RefusalDiagnosticsTest` reported "the premise of this test is gone: the write did not survive". FK checks
bypass RLS, so the row was genuinely gone: the other runner's `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` landed between the
insert and its use. The symptom presents as *a write made inside `withTenant()` that did not survive* —
H-B's family, and the "correct on a clean build, wrong on a machine carrying earlier state" shape this
section records four times. That is why it read as a mystery rather than as a second process.

**The alternative is ruled out rather than left open.** "363 passed" is also what this suite prints when
`BootstrapLoggingTest`'s container-log case skips — the **only** `markTestSkipped` in the suite, which
fires when `docker compose version` is unreachable — and the in-image run two sentences above is exactly
`363 passed, 1 skipped`. So the recorded number had a benign producer that nobody had excluded. It is
excluded now: a skip prints `1 skipped` beside the count and leaves the run green, and the run in question
was red. **And the reason the identity was lost is fixed.** `bin/gate` (`composer gate`) runs the six gate
steps with every line tee'd to `storage/logs/gate-<utc>.log`, because what was recorded for that run was a
one-line summary rather than the output — the reviewer caught run 2 of five in one sitting by redirecting
to a file, and this makes that the default. The serialization itself is **not** fixed here: it is
pre-existing, suite-only, and this round was for the High. `R-049` owns it.

**Four things this task found that no `§5` row named, each fixed where it was found:**

1. **`DB::listen()` fires AFTER execution.** The planning chat's B4 specified a query listener for the
   task's headline invariant. A listener that throws raises the exception and the row is already written
   (`rows written = 1`); `beforeExecuting` leaves `rows written = 0`. B4's mechanism would have made this
   task's own invariant test **green while being false**, which is the most expensive shape of defect
   this project has had. Reproduced inside the suite so the choice cannot be quietly undone.
2. **`log_statement = 'none'` does not cover a statement that FAILS.** `log_min_error_statement`
   defaults to `error` and logs the offending statement in full, so the one path where a human is most
   likely to be reading the log was the one path still publishing the role verifier. Found by asserting
   against the real container log rather than against the setting.
3. **One of this project's own tests published three verifiers per run.** `select rolpassword = ? ...` on
   the root connection logs bound parameters. It now reads the value and compares in PHP.
4. **The Redactor redacted `sqlstate`.** `SECRET_KEYS` is matched by substring and `sqlstate` contains
   `sql`, so the five alphanumerics an operator greps for came out as `[redacted]`. The substring rule is
   not loosened — it is what makes `DB_OWNER_PASSWORD` and `passwordConfirmation` both covered — and there
   is now a one-entry allowlist that a key has to be argued onto.

**Two deliberate trades, stated here rather than left in a commit message:**

- **The tenant-scope guard is armed per REQUEST, not always.** The invariant is about a request, and the
  suite proves the RLS backstop by querying a tenant table with no scope and expecting zero rows
  (`TenantIsolationTest`, `ConnectionReuseTest`, `WorkerScopeTest`). An always-armed guard would have
  needed a suspension helper for those — an escape hatch in the one class that exists to have none. So
  console, scheduled and queue work is left to RLS, which is what it already was.
- **`routes/drain.php` is loaded when it exists and the environment is not production.** A drain is only
  observable with a request that outlives the signal, and no such route belongs in the application. The
  file is mounted into the drain container and never committed, with a test asserting its absence from
  the repository. This is production code reading the filesystem for an optional route file; the
  alternative — a sleep in a middleware behind a header — is a production code path a request can
  trigger, which is strictly worse.

**The `§11` reviews, and the one round of fixes they produced (2026-10-27).** Both reviews ran in separate
sessions with the diff and the ticket only. **32 findings: 0 Critical, 3 High, 13 Medium, 16 Low.** Three
Highs blocked the tag, plus QA's M-6 — the `§6` coverage number, which is a gate requirement whatever its
severity. All four were fixed in one round; everything else went to `§5` with an owner and a date and was
swept into `§3.2` the same day.

| Fix | What was measured before | What is measured now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **QA H-1 / security #5** — the guard reported a pass for paths it never read, and its path list was duplicated | `php guard/bin/guard src nonexistent` printed "C1 and C2 hold across 2 scanned path(s)" and exited **0**, having opened no file: the count was the paths it was HANDED, and a path resolving to neither file nor directory was skipped in silence. The list was spelled out twice — in `guard/bin/guard` and again in `RepoGuardTest` — so the test could agree with its own copy while CI scanned a different set | One source (`SourceGuard::PATHS`), and rule 6: a path that resolves to nothing, or to no `.php` file, is a **violation** reported through the same channel as every other rule. The success line states **files read**: "C1 and C2 hold across 124 file(s) in 6 path(s)". Proven both ways — `definitely-not-a-path-in-this-repo` exits **1** naming `unscannable-path`, and `app/Features` (which holds only `.gitkeep`) is refused as "resolved, but produced no file to read" |
| **Security #1** — non-GET bypassed the limiter and the shedder entirely | `Route::fallback()` registers GET alone, and `RouteCollection::match()` reads only the bucket for the request's own method, so every other verb found no route, fell to `checkForAlternateVerbs`, and the ROUTER answered 405 before any route middleware ran. Measured: `GET /nope` recorded **3** attempts against the unknown bucket while POST, PUT, DELETE and OPTIONS each recorded **0**, and `OPTIONS /nope` was answered **200 `text/html`** with an `Allow` header by the router's own short-circuit | `Route::any()`, which is `Router::$verbs` and therefore cannot go stale when the framework adds a method. All eight verbs counted, measured: GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS and QUERY each record **3** attempts and are refused on the second path. `OPTIONS /healthz` is now a 404 `problem+json` with **no** `Allow` header. The accepted trade is asserted rather than discovered: a wrong verb on a real route is now **404, not 405**, and `ProblemType::MethodNotAllowed` keeps a test because `ProblemRenderer` still maps the thrown exception. The residual — a verb outside `Router::$verbs`, measured as `PROPFIND /nope` → **405, 0 attempts** — is **R-040** |
| **Security #2** — the per-IP bucket was the load balancer's | No `trustProxies()` anywhere, no `config/trustedproxy.php`, and no mention of forwarded headers in `docs/`, `app/`, `bootstrap/` or `config/`. Measured: `REMOTE_ADDR=10.0.0.7` plus `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.9` gave `$request->ip() = 10.0.0.7`, so behind the locked ALB `ratelimit:api:ip:<hash>` collapses to one bucket per ALB node shared by every client — and ~30 unknown-path requests a minute would lock every other client out of the 404 bucket | `config/trustedproxy.php` reading `TRUSTED_PROXIES`, which `TrustProxies` consults per request, plus a sixth boot clause that refuses to serve outside `local`/`testing` on an empty value, on `*`/`**`, and on any entry that is not an address, a CIDR or `REMOTE_ADDR` — because an entry Symfony cannot parse is **inert**, which would restore the bug with nothing in any log. **Not `trustProxies(at: '*')`**, which the review was right to rule out: it makes the header attacker-controlled and the limit bypassable by rotating it, which is worse than the bug. Both directions are tested through a real request against the key the middleware actually wrote: with `10.0.0.0/8` trusted the bucket carries `sha1('198.51.100.23')` and not the proxy's, and with nothing trusted it carries the proxy's — the second case is the measurement the boot check exists for. Proven as a real process too: a production `boot:check` with no `TRUSTED_PROXIES` exits **1** naming `untrusted-proxy-configuration`. **The single-ingress assumption is written down** in `config/trustedproxy.php`: the task accepts traffic from the balancer and from nothing else, and Task 13 owns both the CIDR and the security group that makes it true |
| **QA M-6** — the `§6` coverage number was never produced | Nothing. CI runs `php artisan test --coverage --min=90`, which measures the whole codebase rather than "code this task added", and no figure had been reported | **93.29 % weighted line coverage over this task's `app/` files — 932 of 999 statements across 35 files.** (Re-measured after the `/readyz` trim changed `ReadinessController`; it read 93.31 %, 934/1001, before that edit. Quoting the earlier number against later code would have been the defect this round corrected five times.) Eight files sit below 90 % individually; the two at 0 % are `CheckBootCommand` and `RefreshReadinessCommand`, which are exercised as real subprocesses that in-process coverage cannot see rather than untested. The genuine gap is `ProblemRenderer` at **50 %**, and its uncovered region is coherent: the `AuthenticationException` and `AuthorizationException` mappings and the generic `HttpExceptionInterface` fall-through with `fromStatus()` and `retryAfter()` — none of which has a producer until Task 5 |

**How the coverage figure was produced, and what it does not cover.** `§4a`'s own rule is that coverage is
CI's figure and is not quoted from a local run, so the method matters: there is no coverage driver on the
builder's machine and no way to install one without a password, so the suite was run **inside the pinned
runtime image** (`dunglas/frankenphp:1.13.3-php8.4-bookworm` plus `pcov`, same extension set as the real
Dockerfile) against the composed Postgres 16 and Redis 7, writing clover XML, and the figure was computed
over exactly the files `git diff --name-only main...cb584c3 -- apps/api/app` lists. That run was **363
passed, 1 skipped, 1244 assertions** — the skip is `BootstrapLoggingTest`'s container-log case, which
needs a Docker socket it does not have inside a container, so it slightly understates
`RoleBootstrapper`'s coverage. **Two things the number does not include, stated because a figure without
its scope is the kind of claim `R-025` counts:** `phpunit.xml`'s `<source>` includes `app` only, so
`guard/src` — 630 lines of C1/C2 control logic this task added — is outside the measurement entirely; and
CI's own `--min=90` remains a whole-codebase figure, so it is not the same number as the one above.

**Two claims corrected rather than accepted, because `§5` is for risks and `R-025`'s standing instruction
is that a claim is checked against measurement:** `§2.11`'s per-request cell said the two probes cost "0
Postgres and 0 Redis round trips" and `/readyz` costs one Redis `GET` (security #8 — and it is the sentence
that would have justified leaving the probe group unlimited, which is R-031); and `§0` named `73a5d78` as
this branch's state with "341 passed, 1149 assertions" while the head was fourteen commits later
(security #15). Neither is a `§5` row.

**One finding this round introduced and removed in the same round, recorded because it is the shape this
branch has paid for four times.** `RateLimitTest` drew one random address per test from
`random_int(1, 254)`; adding the eight-verb dataset took the file to ~26 requests, and the birthday bound
made a collision likely rather than rare — two unrelated cases failed with 429 where they expected 200 and
404. Swapping random for a counter made it **worse**: the counter restarts at 1 each run while real Redis
keeps a bucket for a minute, so a second run inside the same minute failed **nine** cases
deterministically. The fix is both halves — a deterministic address so a failure reproduces, and the
buckets **cleared** in `beforeEach` from the limiter's own configured bucket list, so the state the file
depends on is owned rather than hoped to be absent.

**Corrected 2026-10-27 (first review of the fix diff, finding 7).** This paragraph ended "Three
consecutive full-file runs: 60 passed, 226 assertions, identical." `RateLimitTest` is **20 passed, 75
assertions** — 13 `it()` of which one carries the eight-verb dataset — measured three times at
`ae43e12` through both `php artisan test` and `vendor/bin/pest`, identical each time. 60/226 is two tests
and ten assertions short of `tests/Feature/Http` as a whole (**62/236** at that commit), so the figure
looks like the directory, measured earlier in the same round and then quoted as the file. The *identical*
half was true and is kept; the numbers were a true measurement attached to the wrong thing, which is
`QA L-1`'s category in the round that corrected QA L-1.

**The second round, 2026-10-27 — the QA review's fourteen remaining rows, and three fixes the user
accepted.** All fourteen are now dispositioned: **seven became `§5` rows** (R-041 … R-047, swept into
`§3.2`), **two were duplicates** of security rows and share one row each (M-2 → R-031, M-8 → R-033),
**four were false claims corrected in place** rather than risks accepted, and **one was already closed** by
this round's own code.

### The first review of the fix diff, and the round it produced (2026-10-27)

`§11`'s **Round limit** allows a fix diff to be reviewed at most twice. This is the first:
`cb584c3..ae43e12`, read by a session that did not write it, scoped at the fixes rather than the original
diff. **9 findings: 0 Critical, 1 High, 3 Medium, 5 Low.** None falsifies one of Task 2's stated
invariants (`§3.1` row 2) — which matters at the *second* review, not this one, so the merge rule applied
in full and the High was fixed rather than carried. The High and one Medium are fixed here; four went to
`§5` as **R-048 … R-051**, swept into `§3.2` the same day; three were this file's own claims being wrong
again and are corrected in place. A fifth row, **R-052**, came out of writing one of those corrections and
is not a review finding -- it is in `§5` and in `§3.2` with the others.

| Fix | What was measured before | What is measured now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **#1 (High)** — the boot clause refused the two catch-alls it could recognise, not the ones that exist | `CATCH_ALL_PROXIES` was `['*', '**']`. Measured at `ae43e12` with peer **198.51.100.77** (outside any proxy range) and `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.9`: `REMOTE_ADDR` → **ACCEPTED**, `ip()` 203.0.113.9 · `0.0.0.0/0` → **ACCEPTED**, `ip()` 203.0.113.9 · `0.0.0.0/0,::/0` → **ACCEPTED**, `ip()` 203.0.113.9 · `*` → REFUSED. `REMOTE_ADDR` was not merely accepted but **documented as valid** in `config/trustedproxy.php`, asserted as acceptable in `BootChecksTest`, and listed as acceptable in this section. The ground the clause gives for refusing `*` — "it trusts every peer" — is a property all four share | `trustsEveryPeer()`: the two literals, the `REMOTE_ADDR` token (Laravel resolves it to the **current peer**, so the trusted list is whoever is connecting), and **any `/0` prefix** — read against the pinned Symfony's `IpUtils`, where `checkIp4()` matches every v4 peer whatever the base address, so `10.0.0.0/0` is as total as `0.0.0.0/0`, and `checkIp6()` does the same. The prefix is compared as a number, not against the string `'0'`, because Symfony's v4 path reaches "matches everything" twice over — `'0'` short-circuits and `'00'` falls through to a zero-length `substr_compare` — so `/00` is refused too. `isProxyAddress()` now floors the prefix at 1 as well, so clause ORDER is not the only thing stopping a `/0`. Re-run through the same probes: all four **REFUSED**, `10.0.0.0/8` still accepted with `ip()` = the peer. The regression test is a nine-case dataset that asserts the refusal **and** `$request->ip()` through the real middleware for each form, with a v6 peer for `::/0` — because `::/0` against a v4 peer leaves `ip()` as the peer, so a single peer address would have proven one case by accident |
| **#2 (Medium)** — the control read one of the value's two sources | `TrustProxies::setTrustedProxyIpAddresses()` is `$this->proxies() ?: config('trustedproxy.proxies')`, and `proxies()` returns `static::$alwaysTrustProxies` first — what `$middleware->trustProxies(at: ...)` writes. The clause read `config()` only. Measured with the config at `10.0.0.0/8` and `TrustProxies::at('*')`: **no failures reported**, `ip()` = 203.0.113.9 from an untrusted peer. `trustProxies(at: '*')` is the one call a maintainer reaches for and the alternative this very section names as deliberately not taken — so the check was blind to the mistake most likely to be made. QA H-1's shape, one round after QA H-1 | `ResolvedTrustedProxies`, a subclass of `TrustProxies` asked once at boot and never registered as middleware: `(new self)->proxies() ?: config('trustedproxy.proxies')`, which **inherits** the precedence instead of restating it, so a framework reordering follows rather than silently stops mirroring. A subclass rather than reflection for that reason. Measured: config `10.0.0.0/8` + `at('*')` → **REFUSED** naming "every peer", and with the override flushed the same config is accepted, so the case is about the override and not the CIDR |

| To `§5`, with an owner and a date | Severity |
| --- | --- |

| **R-049** — the suite's per-test `TRUNCATE` is unserialized against a second runner, and the failure is a silent lost write. The identified cause of this section's one red run | Medium |
| **R-050** — `/readyz`'s `reason` still publishes which dependency is down, which is R-027's signal, on the one group with no limiter | Low |
| **R-051** — R-031's absence assertions pin three names that were never top-level and omit the three R-031 called sensitive | Low |
| **R-052** — not from the review: found while writing R-050's correction. Scramble publishes a controller's whole docblock as the endpoint's spec `description`, so `docs/openapi.json` carries the shed threshold `0.9 x app_limit` — R-031's own capacity argument, one layer out. The correction was moved into the method body so the fix for one disclosure did not add a second | Low |

### The second review of the fix diff — the cap, and the round it produced (2026-10-27)

`§11`'s **Round limit** allows a fix diff to be reviewed at most twice. **This was the second, so the cap
is reached**: from here only a failure of Task 2's own stated invariant (`§3.1` row 2) blocks the tag, and
everything else goes to `§5` with an owner and a date whatever its severity, including a High. The range was
`ae43e12..79f0882`, read by a session that did not write it. **7 findings: 0 Critical, 0 High, 3 Medium,
4 Low.** The bar was set before the review was commissioned, and **none of the seven falsifies an
invariant**, so nothing blocked.

**The reviewer re-measured the three invariants at head rather than citing them** — which is the previous
round's finding 9 applied by the next reviewer:

| Invariant (`§3.1` row 2) | How it was checked at `79f0882` |
| --- | --- |
| A request without tenant context never reaches the DB | Untouched by the range; suite green |
| Logs carry no SQL, bound parameters or secrets | Redaction untouched; suite green. `R-057` below is adjacent and outside its subject |
| Each named guard fails on a deliberately bad file | **Re-measured, not cited**: a planted `app/Platform/Boot/ZzUnreadableProbe.php` carrying `select set_config(?, ?, false)` at mode 644 produced `1 violation(s)` and error code 1; the tree was then restored clean |

**Two findings were fixed and five went to `§5`, by the user's decision on 2026-10-27**, which also ended
the loop: no third review, gates and push, and the tag after CI (`§1`).

| Fix | What was measured before | What is measured now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **#2 (Medium)** — the clause reads one entry at a time and the trusted set is their **union** | Every refusal in `trustedProxiesAreSound()` tested entries in isolation, so two entries that are each half a family are each sound and together are `*`. Measured at `ae43e12` with `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.9`: `0.0.0.0/1,128.0.0.0/1` → **ACCEPTED**, `ip()` 203.0.113.9 from peer 198.51.100.77 **and** from 255.1.2.3 · `::/1,8000::/1` → **ACCEPTED**, `ip()` 203.0.113.9 from 2001:db8::77. The same bypass as the High the previous round fixed, spelled with two entries instead of one. The nine-case dataset's `'10.0.0.0/8,0.0.0.0/0'` encodes the converse — the widest member — and not this | `unionCoversAFamily()`: entries become packed first/last addresses, grouped by family (4 bytes or 16), sorted and swept for a gap; a list leaving no address of a family uncovered is refused. **Adjacency is the mechanism, not overlap** — `0.0.0.0/1` and `128.0.0.0/1` touch without overlapping, so merging on overlap alone would miss exactly this case. **A sweep and not arithmetic**, because counting a v6 family means 2^128 and therefore `bcmath` or `gmp` on the boot path, which `§6`'s fresh-clone clause will not carry. Re-run through the same probes: all five list forms **REFUSED by this clause** (asserted by message, so the wrong clause firing is a red test), while `10.0.0.0/8,192.168.0.0/16` and a duplicated `10.0.0.0/8` stay **accepted**, because a two-subnet balancer must not be refused |
| **#4 (Low)** — `0.0.0.0/1` was accepted **and pinned as acceptable** | `isProxyAddress()` floored the prefix at 1, and the accepted-case test asserted `0.0.0.0/1` on the stated ground that "a /1 is the narrowest prefix that is still a range rather than the whole internet, so it is the boundary worth pinning". A /1 is half of every address there is: measured, `0.0.0.0/1` with peer 10.0.0.7 gave `ip()` 203.0.113.9. The floor was placed where it excluded nothing | `isWiderThanABalancer()`, with the floor at **/8 for IPv4 and /32 for IPv6** — **not a round number picked at the keyboard**: those are the widest values this project already documents as legitimate (`10.0.0.0/8` in `.env.example`'s guidance and in the accepted set, `2001:db8::/32` beside it), and an AWS VPC IPv4 CIDR is /16–/28, so Task 13's real value sits well inside. A value outside the floor is a decision rather than a CIDR: one edit, with a reason. **The boundary is pinned from both sides** — /7 and /31 refused, /8 and /32 accepted — and the one accepted-case assertion that moved is the one the finding named |

| To `§5`, with an owner and a date | Severity |
| --- | --- |
| **R-053** — `ResolvedTrustedProxies` mirrors two of the **three** sources `proxies()` reads: a `TrustProxies` subclass setting `$proxies` is accepted while `ip()` is the caller's own header | Medium |
| **R-054** — `bin/gate` keeps the failing test's identity but **not the stack it promises**, and the test step is still one line | Medium |
| **R-055** — the malformed-entry clause's stated mechanism is wrong for three forms: two make `$request->ip()` throw, two are total catch-alls | Low |
| **R-056** — `R-052` names one leak in the published `/readyz` description; the same description publishes four more | Low |
| **R-057** — the gate transcript writes unredacted SQL and bound parameters into `storage/logs/`, beside the application's own channels | Low |

| Corrected rather than accepted | What was wrong |
| --- | --- |
| **finding 1's claim half** | `ResolvedTrustedProxies`' docblock asserted "THERE ARE TWO SOURCES FOR ONE VALUE" and that it "reads the same storage the real middleware writes". `proxies()` is `static::$alwaysTrustProxies ?: $this->proxies` — **three** sources, and this subclass reads its own null `$proxies`, never the one on the instance the stack runs. The docblock now enumerates all three and marks the one it does not see, `BootChecks::configuredProxies()` says so too, and the residual is `R-053`. A false claim is corrected, not accepted (`R-025`) |

**What the review confirmed rather than found**, recorded so nobody re-walks it: per **entry** the catch-all
clause is now complete against the pinned Symfony — no single entry other than a `/0` matches every peer of
a family, because `checkIp4()` short-circuits only on netmask `0` and `checkIp6()` returns false below 1, so
the four spellings are the four; `remote_addr` in lower case is refused as malformed and leaves `ip()` as
the peer, which matches Laravel's own case-sensitive `'REMOTE_ADDR'` comparison; the framework's fourth
source (`is_null($trustedIps) && (laravel_cloud() || $onForgeOrVapor)` → `'*'`, reachable through the
attacker-controlled `Host`) is blocked by the empty-value refusal, **incidentally**, since that clause exists
for the one-bucket-per-ALB bug; `docker/Caddyfile` sets no `trusted_proxies`, so there is no fifth source at
the server layer; and `Illuminate\Http\Middleware\TrustProxies` is index 3 of the resolved global stack, the
framework's own class, verified by reflection rather than from `bootstrap/app.php`'s comment.

**Claims re-run rather than read**, since nine had been corrected across the two previous rounds and the
reviewer was told a wrong claim is a finding of the same severity as the thing it misdescribes. All of these
held: `371 passed / 1280 assertions` at `79f0882`; `composer gate` green, exit 0, six steps; `RateLimitTest`
**20/75** and `tests/Feature/Http` **62/236**, so the previous round's finding 7 was right; `932/999 =
93.29 %` and "35 `app/` files" = the added-or-modified set `task-1..ae43e12` **exactly** (30 added + 5
modified); `R-048` reproduced (mode 000 → a pass line and exit 0 with only a PHP warning, at 126 files now
that `ResolvedTrustedProxies.php` exists); `R-049`'s statics (six tables, `TRUNCATE … RESTART IDENTITY
CASCADE` on `pgsql_owner`, `pg_advisory_lock` only in `RoleBootstrapper`); `R-051`'s nesting claim **exact**
at `cb584c3`; `openapi:check` fails on any change to the committed document (exit 1); the ledger arithmetic
48→53 and 2+15+35+1. **Unverifiable from a session, and disclosed as such by `§0` itself:** the 93.29 %
figure — there is no `pcov` and no `xdebug` on this machine, confirmed.

**Gate at the fix, `composer gate`, all six steps green, exit 0: 381 passed / 1321 assertions** (+10 tests
and +41 assertions over `79f0882`, which is the two datasets and the widened accepted set). Full output
kept at `storage/logs/gate-<utc>.log` per `R-049`'s control — with `R-054` recording what that file does and
does not hold.

**CI's verdict on the merge commit, 2026-10-27: `2e42267`, all three jobs green.** `Secret scan`,
`Pint, PHPStan, Pest`, and `FrankenPHP runtime — SIGTERM drain and a Redis outage` — the three W-3 named.
**Reported by the user rather than read here**, and recorded as such: CI conclusions live on GitHub, this
environment has no `gh` and no token, and `§4a`'s rule is that it carries what was measured along with who
measured it. That closes **W-3**, the last open item on this task.

With it, `§6`'s precondition for Task 3 is satisfied **on both halves, each measured rather than assumed**:

| `§6` requires | State | How it was established |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the previous task **tagged** | `task-2` → `2e42267`, local and remote | verified in the repo — and `git diff task-2 2e42267` is empty, so the tag certifies the tree it names, which was **not** true of the tag as first pushed (see `§0.1 W-7`) |
| the previous task **green** | all three jobs green on `2e42267` | CI, reported by the user 2026-10-27 |

**The two land on the same commit, which is the point.** `2e42267` is both the squash merge and the commit
the tag resolves to, so "green" and "tagged" are statements about one tree rather than about two commits
that look alike — the failure mode the tag correction earlier that day was an instance of, and the one
`task-1` paid a day for when `3fe10c2` looked un-green on `main` because run #27 had been superseded.
**A `Cancelled` run is not a pass; this is a pass.**

| **R-001** | **`main` is not actually protected.** GitHub rulesets are not enforced on a private repository under an individual plan ("won't be enforced on this private repository until you move to GitHub Team"). Branch protection is configured but inert, so a direct push to `main` succeeds and can bypass PR review.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Medium                                      | Single developer. The core of `PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md §11` is that QA and Security reviewers run in **separate agent sessions without the builder's reasoning** — that is enforced by process, not by a GitHub button. CI runs on every push to `main` (proven), so a broken merge is caught within a minute. Paying for GitHub Team before the first paying tenant is not justified.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **Working rule, binding:** one task = one branch = one PR. Never commit to `main` directly. Never merge until both required checks are green and the §11 QA + Security reviews have run. Squash merge only.<br><br>**⚠ BOTH HALVES WERE BROKEN AT THEIR FIRST USE, `task-1`, 2026-10-26 — recorded in §4a.** The merge was a merge commit, not a squash; and the merge happened before CI was looked at, with the run on `main` for the merged commit (`3fe10c2`, run #27) still not green.<br><br>**⚠ AND THE SQUASH HALF WAS BROKEN AGAIN THE SAME DAY, ON PR #2** — the workflow fix below, which was created on a branch and sent through a PR precisely to be the first clean exercise of this rule after it failed. It merged as a **merge commit**: `c07bae6` has two parents and `296db14` is in `main`'s history as a distinct commit. The _verify-before-merge_ half was performed this time; the _squash_ half was not. Twice out of two uses, the half that fails is the one no tooling checks — which is the argument for either enforcing it (an organisation ruleset, this row's review trigger) or dropping the clause rather than carrying a rule that is not followed. **This row is the reason an unenforced `main` was accepted at all:** rulesets are inert on a private individual-plan repo, so the control is entirely this rule being performed. A control that is not performed is not a control.<br><br>**What stops it recurring at Task 2's merge — three things, in order of how much they rely on anyone remembering:**<br>**(a) ✅ LANDED 2026-10-26 — PR #2, merged as `c07bae6`.** `ci.yml`'s `cancel-in-progress` is now `${{ github.ref != 'refs/heads/main' && !startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/') }}` instead of `true`, so it no longer applies to `main` or to any tag: the CI-minute saving stays on PR branches, where `github.ref` is `refs/pull/N/merge`, and **every commit on `main` now produces a verdict**. This was the part that made the `task-1` failure invisible rather than merely unobserved. **Mechanical; needs nobody to remember anything** — which is why it was the one worth landing first.<br>**(a2) ✅ LANDED — the setting is applied and verified 2026-10-27; decided 2026-10-26 after the clause went 0 for 2.** Settings → General → Pull Requests: **disable merge commits and rebase merging, leave squash enabled.** GitHub then offers no other button, so the clause stops depending on anyone remembering it at the moment of merging. Same shape as the CI fix above, and like it, **it needs no organisation plan** — repository merge-method settings are available on a private individual-plan repo, unlike the rulesets this row's main risk is about. The two breaches it follows: `task-1` merged as `3fe10c2` (a merge commit), and **PR #2 merged as `c07bae6`, also a merge commit — the PR created specifically to be the first clean exercise of this rule.** Both have two parents and both left their branch commits in `main`'s history, so neither was squashed. The clause is not dropped and not left to memory; it is made unavailable to break. **Applied and verified 2026-10-27, which makes this the second of the three to be both mechanical and confirmed in use:** the merge button on PR #3 read **Squash and merge** with **no dropdown** offering the other two (W-2's own *how verified*), and PR #3 landed as **`2e42267` with one parent**, measured in the repo rather than inferred from the button. **The clause's record is now two breaches in three uses — and the third could not have gone the other way.**<br>**(b) Verify the right run.** A PR's own checks run against `refs/pull/N/merge`, a _different commit_ from the merge commit that lands on `main`. "Both required checks green" was satisfiable on PR #1 and still left the merged state unverified. The rule now means: the run for the **merge commit on `main`**, by sha — and the `task-N` tag goes on only after that run is green, not before.<br>**✅ SETTLED 2026-10-26 by the user: the rule means CODE, and the exception is now stated rather than performed silently.** It had been broken all day by documentation commits — `28f7953`, `cdadb43`, `8ed6519` and others went straight to `main`, and the §4a record of this task was written by commits that broke the rule it records. The resolution, with its boundary:<br>**A documentation-only commit — `docs/**`, including `PLAN.md`and`PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`— goes straight to`main`.** No branch, no PR. A doc-only branch per edit is real friction on files updated several times per task, and §0 in particular has to be editable the moment state changes.<br>**Any commit touching `apps/`, `.github/`or any file outside`docs/`needs a branch and a PR, whatever its size.** The boundary is the path, not the diff: a one-line workflow change is code. The`ci.yml` concurrency fix was correctly given a branch and PR #2 on exactly this reasoning, before the rule said so.<br>What this closes is not the breaches but the _silence_ about them: an exception that is performed and not written down is how the two breaches of the **squash** half went unnoticed until someone counted. Note the interaction with **(a2)**: disabling merge commits does nothing about direct pushes, which only a branch-protection ruleset would stop — the organisation-plan dependency in (c). Until then the doc exception is also the only part of this row that cannot be broken by accident, because it permits what was happening anyway.<br>**(c) Nothing here is enforced by GitHub, and that does not change until the repo moves to an organisation** (the review column of this row). Until then (a) is the only part that is not a promise. | Muhammad                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | At client handover (Transfer Ownership to an Organization, where rulesets are enforced) **or** when a second developer joins — whichever is first.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| **R-009** | **The role password verifier is written to the Postgres log on every bootstrap.** F4 moved the _plaintext_ off the wire; it did not stop a credential reaching the log. Measured across one `db:bootstrap`: 110 new container-log lines, **0** occurrences of the plaintext and **6** of `SCRAM-SHA-256$4096:…` — twice per role, once as the bound parameter `format()` sends and once in the `ALTER ROLE` statement text. The verifier is the complete `pg_authid` secret: `ServerKey` lets an attacker complete a SCRAM handshake **as the server** (and `sslmode=require` does not authenticate the server — R-008), and `StoredKey` enables offline password recovery at 4096 PBKDF2-SHA256 rounds. M-1, scoped security review of the task-1 fixes.                                                                                                | Medium                                      | `log_statement=all` is local-only (R-004) and Postgres is bound to `127.0.0.1`; the exposure in a deployed environment depends on a log configuration that does not exist yet. The verifier is strictly less dangerous than the plaintext it replaced — it is not password-equivalent for client authentication — so this is a reduction, not a regression. The fix is a one-line `SET log_statement = 'none'` on the bootstrap session, which was measured to suppress it completely, but it belongs with the task that decides what the application logs at all. Task 2 (2026-10-26).** The depth counter is in the code and was already there at `7f1c8f6`: `TenantContext::$openScopes` is `array<string,int>`, incremented in the transaction body and decremented-then-dropped in the `finally`, so an inner scope's exit restores the outer scope's record rather than erasing it. The row text above describes the boolean it replaced and is stale as written (B3 of `QRPAGER-9`). The lint half shipped with R-010 — `Guard\SourceGuard` rejects a raw `set_config` inside a callback, which is the vector this row depends on — and F4 moved to Task 4. **Accepted as a Medium owned by Task 2 on 2026-10-26, decided by the user, with R-010.** The decision names the fix that is **forbidden**: _do not record the active tenant beside the `$openScopes` entry._ That is a PHP mirror of tenant scope, it is exactly what H-A was, and reintroducing it in the last round of a branch this long is how H-A comes back — the fifth round removed the mirror precisely because a mirror that agrees today diverges tomorrow. **At Task 2's acceptance gate**, with R-010: `$openScopes` becomes a depth counter (or re-asserts its entry value rather than unsetting) — neither of which stores a tenant id — and the lint guard that makes the vector unreachable ships with a deliberately-bad fixture proving it fails. **Also closes F4 of the seventh review**, which has the same premise: inside a live scope, after a raw `set_config` to another tenant's uuid, `current()` returns that uuid — two uuids are indistinguishable to a format check — and a Redis key already written from that answer is not rolled back by the end-of-block refusal. Closing it needs the tenant recorded beside the scope entry, which is the PHP mirror H-A was, so it belongs with the depth-counter change and not in a fix commit |

So an adversary with any means of degrading Redis converts a rate-limited API into an unlimited one, and the only trace is a warning line per request. Measured 2026-10-26: `core.hooksPath` unset, `.git/hooks/pre-commit` absent, so no commit on this branch was ever scanned before it was written. Found while checking where a pasted GitHub PAT had reached, and the second hook was found by the first commit that tried to describe the first: the `commit-msg` hook rejected it for a 83-character subject. **Measured consequence: 8 of this branch's 36 subjects exceed the 72-character limit the hook enforces**, including `fix(api): close R-022 — a connection goes back to the pool clean, both ways a request ends` at 90. The CI job is the backstop that actually held: it scans full history, so a secret that reached a commit would fail the build before a merge, which is the property `§10`'s "zero secrets in history, ever" is really about. Worth knowing for what it implies, though: a GitHub squash merge writes its commit **server-side**, where no local hook runs, so this rule's enforcement on `main` rests on the person typing the subject, which is R-001's ground and not this row's. | Task 12 (`QRPAGER-19`), which owns the SAST/DAST and secret-scanning pass | **At Task 12's acceptance gate**: either the hook activation is mechanical — a setup script, or a check that fails when `core.hooksPath` is unset — or the row is re-accepted with the CI job named as the only real control for the secret half and nothing at all named for the subject half.
