| **R-029** | **A proof's own check reported a conclusion it had not established.** The SIGTERM drain proof asserted the image's extensions with five `docker compose exec … php -m \| grep -qix <ext>` pipelines under `set -euo pipefail`. `grep -q` closes the pipe on its first match, so the producer writes into a closed stream, and `pipefail` turns any producer-side failure or partial read into the same verdict the check uses for absence — `extension [redis] is missing from the image`. It failed that way on the first CI run of `task-2-api-core` against an image that was correct. Measured 2026-10-26: `extension_loaded("redis")` returns **PRESENT** on the same image where a short read reports **MISSING**; `pdo_pgsql` is line **21** of `php -m` and `redis` is line **27**, so a stream cut short finds the first and misses the second, which is exactly the failure CI reported. | Low | The failure direction is **false alarm only**, and that was reasoned rather than assumed: `grep -q` matching is positive evidence, so a truncated read can invent an absence but not a presence — the check could fail a good image and could not pass a bad one. So nothing shipped on a wrong verdict. It is a row rather than a silent fix because the CLASS is worse than the instance: a check that cannot distinguish "I failed to measure" from "the thing is absent" will, in another place, fail in the direction that matters. This is the same family as the unproven controls `§4a` keeps recording — a control asserting more than it observed — and not R-016's "correct on a clean build, wrong on a machine carrying earlier state", which was the first hypothesis and which the git history rules out: the Dockerfile's extension line has only ever had one form. | Replaced by **one** exec that asks PHP directly and prints a `MISSING=` sentinel, so the three outcomes are separate messages — proven: a genuinely absent extension gives "extensions genuinely missing from the image: no_such_ext", a stopped container gives "a failure to MEASURE, not a missing extension", and the real image gives all five as `present` in the log. The CI job also now builds once with `--no-cache --pull` before either proof, and `runtime_start` passes `--pull`, so freshness is mechanical rather than hoped for. | Task 12 (`QRPAGER-19`), which owns the CI security and quality pass | **At Task 12's acceptance gate**: sweep the other shell-based gates for the same shape — a pipeline whose producer-side failure is indistinguishable from the thing it is testing for. `bin/openapi-check`, `bin/redis-down-proof`'s curl assertions and CI's Postgres-log grep are the candidates. |

| **R-031** | **`/readyz` published capacity telemetry to any anonymous caller, with no limiter, while `ShedLoad` withheld the same figure on principle.** Measured body: `{"ready":true,...,"probe":{"postgres_up":true,"redis_up":true,"app_backends":0,"app_limit":40,"saturation":0,"measured_at":...},"probe_age_seconds":0,"freshness_budget_seconds":15}`. `ShedLoad` keeps `saturation` out of its 503 body because "it tells a caller how close this task is to the edge, which is a capacity measurement of somebody else's infrastructure" -- and `ShedLoadTest:87` asserts that absence -- while the same number was published continuously, at higher precision, on demand, on the one middleware group that carries no rate limiter. The shed threshold is `0.9 x app_limit`, so a caller read `app_limit`, watched `app_backends` climb, calibrated to the exact onset of shedding and confirmed the effect in real time; and `redis_up` published the signal R-027 says means rate limiting is currently off. **The second half of that sentence is corrected in `R-050` (2026-10-27): removing `redis_up` did not remove that signal, because `reason` still names the dependency on the same endpoint. What the trim removed is the capacity figures.** **Found independently by both reviews** -- QA M-2 and security #3 -- which is the first time this project's two reviewers converged on the same finding from different checklists. | Medium | **CLOSED, so this cell records what the trade was rather than why it was carried.** The probe group being **empty** is correct and stays: it is the mechanism behind "health and readiness keep answering", and a limiter on it would let the balancer kill a task that is merely busy, which `§4 › Edge & ops` calls a cascade rather than shedding. So the fix was always the **body**, never the exemption -- and the question was whether `reason` could survive the trim, because it is what makes a 503 diagnosable at 3am. It could: `reason` names which dependency failed without quantifying anything — and naming it is a disclosure that was kept deliberately, not one the trim removed, which is `R-050`. | The two figures remain where an operator needs them and a caller cannot reach them: `ShedLoad` logs its saturation reason at warning, and the carrier prints the snapshot it wrote. | Task 2 -- closed here | **CLOSED at Task 2.** The body is now `ready`, `reason` and `probe_age_seconds`; `probe`, `app_backends`, `app_limit`, `saturation`, `postgres_up`, `redis_up`, `measured_at` and `freshness_budget_seconds` are gone. The spec was regenerated and `OpenApiSpecTest` asserts the three present fields **and the absence of the five removed names** -- narrower than this claimed, and corrected by `R-051`: three of the five were nested under `probe`, and `postgres_up`, `redis_up` and `measured_at` are not in the list at all, so the `probe` assertion is what carries it; `RouteSurfaceTest` asserts `probe` is missing rather than null. `probe_age_seconds` is kept deliberately -- it discloses only carrier liveness and it is what separates "this task has no snapshot" from "its carrier died". |

---

## 6. Open items

- Billing / subscriptions: not in the Blueprint and not requested by the client. **Out of scope until stated.**
- Launch tier: unset by design until Task 14.
- **Queue transport for push dispatch.** The Blueprint specified SQS. The design is an outbox table plus a worker, which satisfies the guarantee — "ready" commits instantly, the send happens behind it with retries — independently of transport. Laravel's Redis queue is the default. **Task 13 decides whether the launch tier needs SQS instead, and Task 14 measures it.** Do not assume either.
- **Data migrations under FORCE RLS — settled, and corrected.** The pattern is `PLAN.md §2.10`. It was wrong until 2026-10-25 (H-1, QA review of task-1): it opened by enumerating tenants with a plain owner read, which returns **zero rows** under FORCE RLS, so the loop body never ran and a backfill would have silently done nothing. The list now comes through one audited `withoutTenantScope()` read; the per-tenant work still needs no bypass. `DataMigrationPatternTest` proves both halves, including at transaction level 1, which is where every migration body runs.
- **`outbox` and `audit_log` retention is unassigned.** Both grow without bound (§2.11): no grant deletes a `sent` or `dead` outbox row, and `audit_log` is append-only, so pruning it can only ever be a partition drop, never a `DELETE`. Nothing breaks before launch; it is a disk bill that grows. **Decide in Task 13 (storage sizing) and measure in Task 14.** Do not let the first person who notices invent a `DELETE`, because the trigger will refuse it.
- **Platform-admin cross-tenant reads are not on the §4 named list.** Task 6 builds a platform dashboard, a restaurants list and an audit-log viewer, all of which read across tenants. `§4 › Tenancy` names exactly four cross-tenant paths and says "adding one requires editing this file first". Task 1 deliberately built **no** policy for these: `qrpager_app` has no cross-tenant policy anywhere. Task 6 must either get §4 amended (a rank-2 edit, so the user's call) or go through §2.8's `SECURITY DEFINER` recipe with its own authorisation. **Raised now so Task 6 does not discover it mid-wizard.**
- **Platform-scope `audit_log` rows (`tenant_id` NULL) can currently be written only through the owner bypass.** `app` holds `INSERT` but the isolation policy's `WITH CHECK` compares `tenant_id` to the context, and `NULL = x` is never true. Task 6's "platform admin actions all land in `audit_log`" therefore needs a decision: an `INSERT`-only policy allowing `tenant_id IS NULL` (write-only for the app, readable only through §2.8), or every platform action naming a tenant. Not invented here.
- **Laravel wraps the driver error — settled at Task 2 (`2655bdd`, wired in at `ccca185`).** `QueryException::getCode()` is not reliably the SQLSTATE, and the shape of the unreliability is now measured rather than warned about: for STATEMENT errors `getCode()` and `getPrevious()->errorInfo[0]` **agree** — `42P01`, `22P02`, `22012` — which is exactly why a `getCode()`-based reader looks correct. For a CONNECTION-level failure they do not: a refused connection and a wrong password both give `getCode()` = the integer **`7`** where the SQLSTATE is **`08006`**. So anything built on `getCode()` printed "SQLSTATE 7" for precisely the failures an operator most needs named — and `08006` is also how a saturated role `CONNECTION LIMIT` arrives at the client, never `53300`. `App\Platform\Database\DriverError` is the single reader: `sqlState()` walks the chain for a `PDOException` with an `errorInfo`, and `sqlStateForMessage()` returns a real SQLSTATE or an empty string rather than a wrong code. `tests/TestCase.php::sqlState()` keeps the same shape for the suite.

### API versioning — the policy for a breaking change

Written here at Task 2, which the `§3` gate requires ("the versioning policy for a breaking change is
written into §6") and `D6` places in this file rather than in `PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md §6`: it is a
project decision, not a universal rule, so it belongs at rank 3.

**The surface.** Every public API route is under `/v1`, served by `bootstrap/app.php`'s
`apiPrefix: 'v1'`. Enforced by `tests/Feature/Http/RouteSurfaceTest.php`, which fails on any public
route outside `/v1` that is not in a written allowlist.

**The two exceptions, and why they are not versioned.** `/healthz` and `/readyz`. A load balancer's
health-check path is infrastructure configuration, and versioning it means the target group has to be
reconfigured because the API's major version changed — a deploy-time coupling with no benefit. They are
on their own middleware group, carrying no rate limiter, no tenant middleware and no load shedding,
which is the mechanism behind `§4 › Edge & ops`'s "while health and readiness keep answering". Approved
by the user on 2026-10-26, after confirming that nothing called the route being replaced: Laravel's
`/up` was referenced in exactly one place outside `vendor/` — `bootstrap/app.php` — and by no
healthcheck, no CI step, no test and no document.

**A published path is never changed in place.** Once a path under `/v1` has been deployed, its
behaviour may only be extended compatibly. The following are **breaking** and may not be done to a
published path:

| Breaking | Compatible |
| --- | --- |
| removing or renaming a field in a response | adding a field |
| narrowing an accepted input, or making an optional field required | widening an accepted input, or adding an optional field |
| changing a field's type, or the meaning of a value | adding a value to an enum **only** where the client is specified to ignore unknown values |
| changing a status code for an existing outcome | adding a status code for a new outcome |
| removing a route, or changing what a path matches | adding a route |

**How a breaking change ships.** A new prefix — `/v2` — carrying only the routes that change. `/v1`
keeps working until it is retired. The two live side by side; there is no "upgrade in place" path, and
no version negotiation by header: the version is in the path, where a CDN, a log line and a WAF rule
can all see it. Retiring `/v1` is its own decision with its own notice period, taken when a tenant is
actually on `/v2`.

**The enum caveat is not theoretical.** `pager_status` is a shared contract generated into
`packages/contracts` at Task 8 (ADR-003) from the PHP enum. Adding a case is a **breaking** change to
any client that switches exhaustively on it, which a TypeScript client does by default. So a new
`pager_status` value is a `/v2` change unless the client contract states that unknown values map to a
documented fallback — a decision for the task that adds the case, recorded with it.

**What enforces this.** The committed OpenAPI document. CI regenerates it from the routes and fails on
a diff, so a breaking change cannot reach `main` without appearing in a reviewed spec diff. The
document is the record of what was published; this policy is what may be done to it.

### Deliberately different from the Blueprint — recorded so nobody "fixes" it

| The Blueprint said                                         | We do not                              | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Split the notification service from the API                | One Laravel application                | That split existed to stop SSE connection load from taking down order handling. **O2 removed SSE.** Web Push needs no open connection, so the load it protected against does not exist. Revisit only if Task 14 measures API contention. |
| Alarms and dashboards in CloudWatch, X-Ray **and Grafana** | CloudWatch + X-Ray only                | Three observability surfaces for one product is three places to look during an incident. Add Grafana only if a named need appears.                                                                                                       |
| Redis in cluster mode, with a channel per instance         | Single ElastiCache, no pub/sub fan-out | That design existed to fan SSE events out to every instance holding connections. **O2 removed it.** Cluster mode for the status projection is a **Task 13 sizing decision measured in Task 14**, not an architectural one.               |

### Deliberately excluded — do not re-add without editing this file

Recorded because each appears in the source material and will otherwise be
"rediscovered" as a missing feature by a future chat.

| Excluded                                                                                                                  | Where it came from                   | Why it is out                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SSE hub, `Last-Event-ID`, heartbeat, connection caps, 503 shedding on a stream, Redis pub/sub with a channel per instance | Blueprint realtime design            | Cancelled by **O2**. No persistent connection at launch.                                                            |
| Wait-time prediction per tenant                                                                                           | "Ideas that would make it unique"    | An idea, never agreed scope                                                                                         |
| Multi-channel fallback: push → SMS → WhatsApp                                                                             | Same                                 | Same. Would also reintroduce storing phone numbers, which `§4 › Data` forbids.                                      |
| POS webhooks / `/orders/:ref/ready` integration                                                                           | Same, and a reference repo's feature | An idea, never agreed scope                                                                                         |
| Group pagers and table pagers                                                                                             | Same                                 | An idea, never agreed scope                                                                                         |
| Testcontainers                                                                                                            | Blueprint testing section            | Superseded: tests run against the real composed Postgres and Redis, which is the same guarantee with less machinery |
